@@ -20,6 +20,87 @@ import numba
 import operator
 from numba.np import numpy_support
 
+
+# Mode strings accepted by @stencil for out-of-bounds handling.
+VALID_STENCIL_MODES = ('constant', 'wrap', 'nearest', 'reflect', 'symmetric')
+MODE_STR_TO_INT = {mode: i for i, mode in enumerate(VALID_STENCIL_MODES)}
+
+
+@register_jitable
+def _stencil_mode_index(index, shape, mode):
+    """Return the adjusted index for one dimension according to *mode*.
+
+    A return value of -1 indicates that the access is out-of-bounds and
+    ``cval`` should be used instead (constant mode and reflect/symmetric
+    fallback).
+    """
+    if mode == 0:       # constant
+        if index < 0 or index >= shape:
+            return -1
+        return index
+    elif mode == 1:     # wrap
+        return index % shape
+    elif mode == 2:     # nearest
+        if index < 0:
+            return 0
+        elif index >= shape:
+            return shape - 1
+        return index
+    elif mode == 3:     # reflect (mirror without repeating the edge)
+        if index < 0:
+            index = -index
+        if index >= shape:
+            index = 2 * (shape - 1) - index
+        if index < 0 or index >= shape:
+            return -1
+        return index
+    elif mode == 4:     # symmetric (mirror with repeating the edge)
+        if index < 0:
+            index = -index
+        if index >= shape:
+            index = 2 * shape - 1 - index
+        if index < 0 or index >= shape:
+            return -1
+        return index
+    else:
+        return -1
+
+
+_STENCIL_GETITEM_DISPATCHERS = {}
+
+
+def _make_stencil_getitem_func(ndim):
+    """Create a Python function implementing mode-aware getitem for ndim dims."""
+    args = ["arr", "idx"]
+    for d in range(ndim):
+        args.append("mode%d" % d)
+    args.append("cval")
+    lines = []
+    for d in range(ndim):
+        lines.append("shape%d = arr.shape[%d]" % (d, d))
+        if ndim == 1:
+            index_expr = "idx"
+        else:
+            index_expr = "idx[%d]" % d
+        lines.append("idx%d = _stencil_mode_index(%s, shape%d, mode%d)" %
+                     (d, index_expr, d, d))
+        lines.append("if idx%d < 0:" % d)
+        lines.append("    return cval")
+    lines.append("return arr[%s]" % ", ".join("idx%d" % d for d in range(ndim)))
+    src = "def _stencil_getitem_%dd(%s):\n    %s\n" % (
+        ndim, ", ".join(args), "\n    ".join(lines))
+    ns = {}
+    exec(src, globals(), ns)
+    return ns["_stencil_getitem_%dd" % ndim]
+
+
+def _get_stencil_getitem_dispatcher(ndim):
+    if ndim not in _STENCIL_GETITEM_DISPATCHERS:
+        func = _make_stencil_getitem_func(ndim)
+        _STENCIL_GETITEM_DISPATCHERS[ndim] = numba.njit(func)
+    return _STENCIL_GETITEM_DISPATCHERS[ndim]
+

+    def get_mode(self, ndim):
+        """Return the per-dimension mode strings for an array with *ndim* dims."""
+        if isinstance(self.mode, str):
+            return [self.mode] * ndim
+        # mode is a tuple/list of strings
+        if len(self.mode) != ndim:
+            raise NumbaValueError(
+                "%d dimensional mode specified for %d dimensional input array" %
+                (len(self.mode), ndim))
+        return list(self.mode)
+
+    def get_mode_ints(self, ndim):
+        return [MODE_STR_TO_INT[m] for m in self.get_mode(ndim)]
+
+    def all_constant(self, ndim):
+        return all(m == 'constant' for m in self.get_mode(ndim))
+
+    @property
+    def cval(self):
+        return self.options.get('cval', 0)

COMMAND:
cd /app && nl -ba docs/source/user/stencil.rst | sed -n '136,200p'

   136	Stencil decorator options
   137	=========================
   138	
   139	.. note::
   140	   The stencil decorator may be augmented in the future to provide additional
   141	   mechanisms for border handling. At present, only one behaviour is
   142	   implemented, ``"constant"`` (see ``func_or_mode`` below for details).
   143	
   144	.. _stencil-neighborhood:
   145	
   146	``neighborhood``
   147	----------------
   148	
   149	Sometimes it may be inconvenient to write the stencil kernel
   150	exclusively with ``Integer`` literals.  For example, let us say we
   151	would like to compute the trailing 30-day moving average of a
   152	time series of data.  One could write
   153	``(a[-29] + a[-28] + ... + a[-1] + a[0]) / 30`` but the stencil
   154	decorator offers a more concise form using the ``neighborhood``
   155	option::
   156	
   157	   @stencil(neighborhood = ((-29, 0),))
   158	   def kernel2(a):
   159	       cumul = 0
   160	       for i in range(-29, 1):
   161	           cumul += a[i]
   162	       return cumul / 30
   163	
   164	The neighborhood option is a tuple of tuples.  The outer tuple's
   165	length is equal to the number of dimensions of the input array.
   166	The inner tuple's lengths are always two because
   167	each element of the inner tuple corresponds to minimum and
   168	maximum index offsets used in the corresponding dimension.
   169	
   170	If a user specifies a neighborhood but the kernel accesses elements outside the
   171	specified neighborhood, **the behavior is undefined.**
   172	
   173	.. _stencil-mode:
   174	
   175	``func_or_mode``
   176	----------------
   177	
   178	The optional ``func_or_mode`` parameter controls how the border of the output array
   179	is handled.  Currently, there is only one supported value, ``"constant"``.
   180	In ``constant`` mode, the stencil kernel is not applied in cases where
   181	the kernel would access elements outside the valid range of the input
   182	array.  In such cases, those elements in the output array are assigned
   183	to a constant value, as specified by the ``cval`` parameter.
   184	
   185	``cval``
   186	--------
   187	
   188	The optional cval parameter defaults to zero but can be set to any
   189	desired value, which is then used for the border of the output array
   190	if the ``func_or_mode`` parameter is set to ``constant``.  The cval parameter is
   191	ignored in all other modes.  The type of the cval parameter must match
   192	the return type of the stencil kernel.  If the user wishes the output
   193	array to be constructed from a particular type then they should ensure
   194	that the stencil kernel returns that type.
   195	
   196	``standard_indexing``
   197	---------------------
   198	
   199	By default, all array accesses in a stencil kernel are processed as
   200	relative indices as described above.  However, sometimes it may be

    89	of the specified stencil kernel is ``float64`` and has thus created the
    90	output array as ``float64`` while the input array is of type ``int64``.
    91	
    92	Stencil Parameters
    93	==================
    94	
    95	Stencil kernel definitions may take any number of arguments with
    96	the following provisions.  The first argument must be an array.
    97	The size and shape of the output array will be the same as that of the
    98	first argument.  Additional arguments may either be scalars or
    99	arrays.  For array arguments, those arrays must be at least as large
   100	as the first argument (array) in each dimension.  Array indexing is relative for
   101	all such input array arguments.
   102	
   103	.. _stencil-kernel-shape-inference:
   104	
   105	Kernel shape inference and border handling
   106	==========================================
   107	
   108	In the above example and in most cases, the array indexing in the
   109	stencil kernel will exclusively use ``Integer`` literals.
   110	In such cases, the stencil decorator is able to analyze the stencil
   111	kernel to determine its size.  In the above example, the stencil
   112	decorator determines that the kernel is ``3 x 3`` in shape since indices
   113	``-1`` to ``1`` are used for both the first and second dimensions.  Note that
   114	the stencil decorator also correctly handles non-symmetric and
   115	non-square stencil kernels.
   116	
   117	Based on the size of the stencil kernel, the stencil decorator is
   118	able to compute the size of the border in the output array.  If
   119	applying the kernel to some element of input array would cause
   120	an index to be out-of-bounds then that element belongs to the border

old = '''.. note::
   The stencil decorator may be augmented in the future to provide additional

old2 = '''``func_or_mode``
----------------

The optional ``func_or_mode`` parameter controls how the border of the output array
is handled.  Currently, there is only one supported value, ``"constant"``.
In ``constant`` mode, the stencil kernel is not applied in cases where
the kernel would access elements outside the valid range of the input
array.  In such cases, those elements in the output array are assigned
to a constant value, as specified by the ``cval`` parameter.

``cval``
--------

The optional cval parameter defaults to zero but can be set to any
desired value, which is then used for the border of the output array
if the ``func_or_mode`` parameter is set to ``constant``.  The cval parameter is
ignored in all other modes.  The type of the cval parameter must match
the return type of the stencil kernel.  If the user wishes the output
array to be constructed from a particular type then they should ensure
that the stencil kernel returns that type.
'''
new2 = '''``func_or_mode`` / ``mode``
---------------------------

The optional ``func_or_mode`` parameter controls how out-of-bounds accesses
are handled when the stencil kernel is applied.  It may be supplied either
positionally or through the ``mode`` keyword::

    @stencil('wrap')
    def kernel_wrap(a):
        return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

    @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
    def kernel_mixed(a):
        return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 0] + a[1, 0]

A single string applies the same mode to every dimension.  A tuple of
strings provides per-dimension control; its length must match the number
of dimensions of the input array.  The supported modes are:

``"constant"`` (default)
    The stencil kernel is not applied where an access would fall outside
    the input array.  Those boundary positions are set to ``cval``.

``"wrap"``
    Out-of-bounds accesses wrap around to the opposite edge of the array.

``"nearest"``
    Out-of-bounds accesses are clamped to the nearest edge element.

``"reflect"``
    Out-of-bounds accesses are mirrored around the edge without repeating
    the edge element.

``"symmetric"``
    Out-of-bounds accesses are mirrored around the edge with the edge
    element repeated.

For ``"reflect"`` and ``"symmetric"``, if the reflected index is still
out-of-bounds (for example because the stencil neighbourhood is larger
than the array), ``cval`` is used for that access.

An invalid mode (or an invalid element of a mode tuple) raises a
``NumbaValueError``.

``cval``
--------

The optional cval parameter defaults to zero but can be set to any
desired value.  It is used as the value written to the border of the
output array in ``"constant"`` mode and as the fallback value for
out-of-bounds accesses in ``"reflect"`` and ``"symmetric"`` modes.
The type of the cval parameter must match the return type of the stencil
kernel.  If the user wishes the output array to be constructed from a
particular type then they should ensure that the stencil kernel returns
that type.
'''
assert old2 in src
src = src.replace(old2, new2, 1)

with open(path, 'w') as f:
    f.write(src)
print("done")

path = 'numba/tests/test_stencils.py'
with open(path) as f:
    src = f.read()

tests = '''

class TestStencilModes(TestStencilBase):
    """Tests for the ``mode`` / ``func_or_mode`` stencil border handling."""

    def test_wrap_1d(self):
        @stencil('wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size
        expected = np.array([a[(i - 1) % n] + a[i] + a[(i + 1) % n]
                             for i in range(n)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_nearest_1d(self):
        @stencil('nearest')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size
        expected = np.array([
            a[max(0, min(i - 1, n - 1))] + a[i] +
            a[max(0, min(i + 1, n - 1))] for i in range(n)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_reflect_1d(self):
        @stencil('reflect')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size

        def reflect(i):
            if i < 0:
                i = -i
            if i >= n:
                i = 2 * (n - 1) - i
            if i < 0 or i >= n:
                return None
            return i

        expected = np.array([
            sum(a[reflect(i + off)] if reflect(i + off) is not None else 0
                for off in (-1, 0, 1)) for i in range(n)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_symmetric_1d(self):
        @stencil('symmetric')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size

        def symmetric(i):
            if i < 0:
                i = -i
            if i >= n:
                i = 2 * n - 1 - i
            if i < 0 or i >= n:
                return None
            return i

        expected = np.array([
            sum(a[symmetric(i + off)] if symmetric(i + off) is not None else 0
                for off in (-1, 0, 1)) for i in range(n)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_constant_default(self):
        @stencil
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        expected = np.array([0., 3., 6., 9., 0.])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_constant_cval(self):
        @stencil('constant', cval=1.0)
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        expected = np.array([1., 3., 6., 9., 1.])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_per_dim_mode(self):
        @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 0] + a[1, 0]

        a = np.arange(6.0).reshape(2, 3)
        expected = np.array([[6., 9., 12.], [3., 6., 9.]])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_mode_keyword(self):
        @stencil(mode='wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size
        expected = np.array([a[(i - 1) % n] + a[i] + a[(i + 1) % n]
                             for i in range(n)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_invalid_mode(self):
        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            @stencil('bogus')
            def kernel(a):
                return a[0]

        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'bogus'))
            def kernel2(a):
                return a[0, 0]

    def test_mode_tuple_length(self):
        @stencil(mode=('wrap',))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[0, 0]

        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            kernel(np.zeros((2, 2)))

    def test_mode_with_neighborhood(self):
        @stencil(mode='wrap', neighborhood=((-1, 1),))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size
        expected = np.array([a[(i - 1) % n] + a[i] + a[(i + 1) % n]
                             for i in range(n)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected)

    def test_mode_with_standard_indexing(self):
        @stencil(mode='wrap', standard_indexing=('b',))
        def kernel(a, b):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1] + b[0]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        b = np.array([10.0])
        expected = np.array([a[(i - 1) % 5] + a[i] + a[(i + 1) % 5] + b[0]
                             for i in range(5)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a, b), expected)

    def test_mode_with_out(self):
        @stencil('wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size
        expected = np.array([a[(i - 1) % n] + a[i] + a[(i + 1) % n]
                             for i in range(n)])
        out = np.zeros(5)
        kernel(a, out=out)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(out, expected)

    def test_reflect_symmetric_fallback_cval(self):
        @stencil('reflect', cval=100.0)
        def reflect_kernel(a):
            return a[-3] + a[0] + a[3]

        @stencil('symmetric', cval=100.0)
        def symmetric_kernel(a):
            return a[-3] + a[0] + a[3]

        a = np.arange(2.0)
        # n=2: reflect -3 and 3 both fall back to cval at every position.
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(reflect_kernel(a),
                                       np.array([200., 101.]))
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(symmetric_kernel(a),
                                       np.array([0., 102.]))

    def test_mode_parallel_fallback(self):
        @stencil('wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

        @njit(parallel=True)
        def call(a):
            return kernel(a)

        a = np.arange(5.0)
        n = a.size
        expected = np.array([a[(i - 1) % n] + a[i] + a[(i + 1) % n]
                             for i in range(n)])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(call(a), expected)


'''
marker = 'if __name__ == "__main__":\n    unittest.main()'
assert marker in src
src = src.replace(marker, tests + marker, 1)
with open(path, 'w') as f:
    f.write(src)
print("done")

    src = f.read()

        mode = \'constant\'  # default style
        func = func_or_mode
    else:
        mode = func_or_mode
        func = None'''

        # A tuple/list of per-dimension modes may be supplied positionally.
        mode = func_or_mode
        func = None
    elif not isinstance(func_or_mode, str):
        mode = \'constant\'  # default style

    else:
        mode = func_or_mode
        func = None'''

+    def _fix_stencil_mode(self, mode):
+        """
+        Extract the mode option from the program IR to provide to
+        StencilFunc.  The mode may be a string or a tuple of strings.
+        """
+        if isinstance(mode, str):
+            return mode
+        mode_def = guard(get_definition, self.func_ir, mode)
+        if isinstance(mode_def, ir.Const):
+            return mode_def.value
+        if mode_def is not None and hasattr(mode_def, 'items'):
+            items = []
+            for item_var in mode_def.items:
+                item_def = guard(get_definition, self.func_ir, item_var)
+                require(isinstance(item_def, ir.Const))
+                items.append(item_def.value)
+            return tuple(items)
+        raise errors.NumbaValueError(
+            "stencil mode should be a string or tuple of strings")

COMMAND:
cd /app && timeout 28 python3 << 'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil

a = np.arange(12.0).reshape(3,4)

def ref(a, mode, cval=0.0):
    n, m = a.shape
    out = np.empty_like(a)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(m):
            def idx(k, s):
                if mode == 'wrap':
                    return k % s
                elif mode == 'nearest':
                    return max(0, min(k, s-1))
                elif mode == 'reflect':
                    if k < 0: k = -k
                    if k >= s: k = 2*(s-1)-k
                    if k < 0 or k >= s: return None
                    return k
                elif mode == 'symmetric':
                    if k < 0: k = -k
                    if k >= s: k = 2*s-1-k
                    if k < 0 or k >= s: return None
                    return k
            i0 = idx(i-1, n); i1 = idx(i, n)
            j0 = idx(j+1, m); j1 = idx(j, m)
            v1 = a[i0,j1] if i0 is not None and j1 is not None else cval
            v2 = a[i1,j1] if i1 is not None and j1 is not None else cval
            out[i,j] = v1 + v2
    return out

for mode in ['wrap','nearest','reflect','symmetric']:
    @stencil(mode)
    def k(a):
        return a[-1,1] + a[0,1]
    res = k(a)
    exp = ref(a, mode)
    print(mode, np.allclose(res, exp), res)
EOF
OUTPUT:
wrap False [[10. 12. 14.  8.]
 [ 6.  8. 10.  4.]
 [14. 16. 18. 12.]]
nearest False [[ 2.  4.  6.  6.]
 [ 6.  8. 10. 10.]
 [14. 16. 18. 18.]]
reflect False [[ 6.  8. 10.  8.]
 [ 6.  8. 10.  8.]
 [14. 16. 18. 16.]]
symmetric False [[ 6.  8. 10. 10.]
 [ 6.  8. 10. 10.]
 [14. 16. 18. 18.]]

import numpy as np

a = np.arange(12.0).reshape(3,4)
@stencil('wrap')
def k(a):
    return a[-1,1] + a[0,1]
print("kernel:\n", k(a))

# manual
n,m = a.shape
out = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(n):
    for j in range(m):
        out[i,j] = a[(i-1)%n, (j+1)%m] + a[i%n, (j+1)%m]
print("manual:\n", out)

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil

a = np.arange(12.0).reshape(3,4)

def ref(a, mode, cval=0.0):
    n, m = a.shape
    out = np.empty_like(a)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(m):
            def idx(k, s):
                if mode == 'wrap':
                    return k % s
                elif mode == 'nearest':
                    return max(0, min(k, s-1))
                elif mode == 'reflect':
                    if k < 0: k = -k
                    if k >= s: k = 2*(s-1)-k
                    if k < 0 or k >= s: return None
                    return k
                elif mode == 'symmetric':
                    if k < 0: k = -k
                    if k >= s: k = 2*s-1-k
                    if k < 0 or k >= s: return None
                    return k
            i0 = idx(i-1, n); i1 = idx(i, n)
            j1 = idx(j+1, m)
            v1 = a[i0,j1] if i0 is not None and j1 is not None else cval
            v2 = a[i1,j1] if i1 is not None and j1 is not None else cval
            out[i,j] = v1 + v2
    return out

for mode in ['wrap','nearest','reflect','symmetric']:
    @stencil(mode)
    def k(a):
        return a[-1,1] + a[0,1]
    res = k(a)
    exp = ref(a, mode)
    print(mode, np.allclose(res, exp))

cd /app && timeout 28 python3 << 'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import njit, stencil

@stencil('wrap')
def k(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

@njit(parallel=True)
def call(a, out):
    k(a, out=out)

a = np.arange(5.0)
out = np.zeros(5)
try:
    call(a, out)

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil

a = np.arange(6.0).reshape(2,3)
@stencil(mode=('wrap', 'constant'), cval=100.0)
def k(a):
    return a[-1,0] + a[0,0] + a[1,0]
print(k(a))
# dim0 wrap, dim1 constant. Since kernel only uses col 0, dim1 constant never OOB -> same as pure wrap on dim0.
print("expected:\n", [[6.,9.,12.],[3.,6.,9.]])

@stencil(mode=('constant', 'wrap'), cval=100.0)
def k2(a):
    return a[0,-1] + a[0,0] + a[0,1]
print(k2(a))
# dim0 constant (kernel only uses row 0, never OOB), dim1 wrap -> same as wrap on dim1.
# row0: col0: a[0,-1]=a[0,2]=2 + a[0,0]=0 + a[0,1]=1 = 3; col1: 0+1+2=3? wait a[0,-1]=a[0,0]=0, a[0,0]=1, a[0,1]=2 => 3; col2: 1+2+0=3
# row1: col0: a[1,-1]=a[1,2]=5 + a[1,0]=3 + a[1,1]=4 = 12; ...
print(k2(a))
EOF

  1005	
  1006	def stencil(func_or_mode='constant', **options):
  1007	    # called on function without specifying mode style
  1008	    if 'mode' in options:
  1009	        if func_or_mode != 'constant' or not isinstance(func_or_mode, str):
  1010	            raise NumbaValueError("The mode option was already specified")
  1011	        mode = options.pop('mode')
  1012	        func = None
  1013	    elif isinstance(func_or_mode, (tuple, list)):
  1014	        # A tuple/list of per-dimension modes may be supplied positionally.
  1015	        mode = func_or_mode
  1016	        func = None
  1017	    elif not isinstance(func_or_mode, str):
  1018	        mode = 'constant'  # default style
  1019	        func = func_or_mode
  1020	    else:
  1021	        mode = func_or_mode
  1022	        func = None
  1023	
  1024	    for option in options:
  1025	        if option not in ["cval", "standard_indexing", "neighborhood"]:
  1026	            raise NumbaValueError("Unknown stencil option " + option)
  1027	
  1028	    wrapper = _stencil(mode, options)
  1029	    if func is not None:
  1030	        return wrapper(func)
  1031	    return wrapper
  1032	
  1033	def _stencil(mode, options):
  1034	    if isinstance(mode, str):
  1035	        if mode not in VALID_STENCIL_MODES:
  1036	            raise NumbaValueError("Unsupported mode style " + mode)
  1037	    elif isinstance(mode, (tuple, list)):
  1038	        if len(mode) == 0:
  1039	            raise NumbaValueError("Mode tuple must not be empty")
  1040	        for m in mode:
  1041	            if m not in VALID_STENCIL_MODES:
  1042	                raise NumbaValueError("Unsupported mode style " + str(m))
  1043	        mode = tuple(mode)
  1044	    else:
  1045	        raise NumbaValueError("Unsupported mode style " + str(mode))
  1046	
  1047	    def decorated(func):
  1048	        from numba.core import compiler
  1049	        kernel_ir = compiler.run_frontend(func)
  1050	        return StencilFunc(kernel_ir, mode, options)
  1051	
  1052	    return decorated
  1053

STEP 181 CALL 1 RETURN 128
COMMAND:
cd /app && git commit -m "Add mode parameter to @stencil for out-of-bounds handling

Support wrap, nearest, reflect, symmetric and constant border-handling
modes in the stencil decorator.  The mode may be supplied as a single
string (applied to every dimension) or as a tuple of strings for
per-dimension control.  Reflect and symmetric modes fall back to cval
when the reflected index is still out of bounds.  Invalid modes and
mode tuples whose length does not match the array dimensionality raise
NumbaValueError.

The mode parameter works together with cval, neighborhood and
standard_indexing.  Non-constant modes are handled by the regular
stencil lowering path; the parallel (parfor) stencil transform falls
back to that path for such modes." && git log --oneline -3 && git status
